**As built (2026-11-18)**, what a later step relies on:

- **The topology**: template-commit-reveal's `offshoot` (6c7bd8b5) names `upstream/with/indexer` (`{"stemBranch": "with/indexer"}`, a root) and `with/indexer` (`{"stem": ["main", "upstream/with/indexer"]}`, merged in that order). `upstream/with/indexer` is jolly-roger `with/indexer` c83f2f6e plus ONE commit of this repo's, its own `.offshoot-divergences` (ad15d0cd): a header and an empty `[upstream/with/indexer]` section. It is needed because a list's last marker names the node to `check-divergences.mjs`, and jolly-roger's list ends in `[with/indexer]`, which at template-commit-reveal names the branch whose stems include this root, so the check compared the node with itself and the root's verify failed `offshoot-divergences.test.ts`. **Any later `upstream/<layer>` root (F3's `upstream/with/messaging`) needs the same file in its first commit**, and every cascade from jolly-roger that changes the layer's list conflicts there: keep the root's.
- **The merge set** was exactly F1a's measured one. Resolutions: this repo's side for `package.json`, `contracts/package.json`, `contracts/tsconfig.json`, `web/package.json`, the lockfile and `impersonate-addresses.json` (each already held jolly-roger's additions under this repo's names; no lockfile changed anywhere, so no `pnpmDepsHash` moved); jolly-roger's for `worker-choice.ts` and its test (F1-9); `README.indexer.md` rewritten (F1-10); `app.ts` and the demo route stay omitted. `host.ts`, `follower.ts`, `contracts/utils/indexer.ts` and their tests were already byte-identical, `indexed.ts` and `contracts/utils/in-process-chain.ts` arrived new; none of the moved files differs from jolly-roger's.
- **Omitted at `with/indexer`** (a section of `.offshoot-omissions`, inherited below): `web/src/lib/onchain/indexer`, `web/test/lib/onchain/indexer.test.ts`, `web/e2e/tests/greeting-counts.e2e.ts` (as F1a predicted), and two it did not: `web/test/lib/indexer/indexed.test.ts` (it folds GreetingsRegistry through `jolly-roger-contracts/utils/in-process-chain.js`, so `indexed.ts` is tested at its home only) and `contracts/deployments/sepolia` (jolly-roger `main`'s records, which `integration` drops).
- **The world test is passed by every caller** (`{isWorld: isEmbeddedChainId}`): template-commit-reveal's `placement/indexer/board.ts` and stratagems' `board/indexed-board.ts`, each through a `boardWorker()` with a test that is red without it (without it the offline world's board was given the live worker; whether a browser suite would have caught that was not measured). infinite-gambit calls `indexerWorkerFor` nowhere. Git's directory-rename detection put the template's new `test/lib/placement/indexer-worker.test.ts` under stratagems' and infinite-gambit's renamed test directories; it is omitted there (their `web/test/lib/placement` entry), and stratagems' own test is named `board-worker.test.ts` so a later cascade of the template's file does not land on it.
- **F1-11, measured**: of `indexed-state.ts`'s 289 lines, about 120 are the plumbing `createIndexed` (jolly-roger's `indexed.ts`, 236) already is: the worker connection (`connect`, line for line), the follower and progress wiring in `start`, the subscriber lifecycle, the shared option types. The rest is commit-reveal's: the block-timestamp cache, the stamped `publish`, the mapping onto `OnchainStateStatus`. A rebuild would also give it three things `indexed.ts` has and it lacks: the progress readable closed on stop, no update after stop, and the `gate`. More than plumbing, so recorded as a K1 item.
- **F1-12's note checked**: the reference game still publishes no processor bundle, so the middleware stays stratagems'.

### Step S1: the push server green and API-stable

Repo: push-notification-server (`~/dev/github/wighawag/push-notification-server`). The user, 2026-11-17: "re push server : ../push-notification-server". It is the server measured on 2026-11-16, unchanged since (`main` 1e0d908 of 2026-08-22, one branch, no worktree), so the "in progress" server of the re-opening is this one and the measurement below stands. Independent of the template tree; the push lane.

**What decides the shape** (origin/main 1e0d908, 2026-08-22): cf-worker only (D1), one `Subscriptions(address, domain, subscriptionID, subscription, timestamp, expiry)` table; `POST /register` with `// TODO authentication of address` (`packages/server/src/api/index.ts:74`), `POST /push` with `// TODO authentication via tokens` (`:99`), not idempotent, `// TODO Retry` for 5xx (`:131`); its tests are one workerd "hello world" and a vacuous `1 + 2`; no AGENTS.md, no work/. Its own findings (`docs/findings/trigger-service-integration.md`) name GAP-1 to GAP-3 and ENHANCEMENT-1, and ask that the ownership proof be ONE implementation shared with the trigger service. missiv already has that primitive: the wallet signs `originPublicKeyPublicationMessage(origin, delegatePublicKey)` and the server recovers the address (`packages/common/src/index.ts:214-216`, `packages/server/src/api/user/index.ts:23-50`). jolly-roger's client keys a subscription by the delegated signer's address (`push-notifications/index.ts:48,148` in the example) and carries the signer's private key unused.

**Decisions** (decided by the user on 2026-11-17: "ok", to S1-1 to S1-6 as proposed again. They were re-opened on 2026-11-16 only because the server's location was unknown, and the location answered is the server they were measured on):

- **S1-1, GAP-1: a token per calling service, scoped to a domain**, compared in constant time, configured by the operator (an env list of `domain:token-hash`). Rejected: a signature per push (a service has no wallet), one global token (a caller for one app could push to another's users).
- **S1-2, GAP-2: `/register` takes the wallet's signature over missiv's publication message and a delegate signature over the request**, so a subscription is keyed by the WALLET address and proven by the delegate key the app already holds. Rejected: keying by the delegate address (a new device is a new key, so the notifier would not know whom to notify); an Ethereum signature per registration by the wallet itself (a wallet prompt per device per app).
- **S1-3, the shared proof's home: a small package extracted from missiv-common**, `missiv`'s repo publishing it (it already implements and tests the message), used by push-notification-server, the notifier (S2) and missiv itself (S3). This answers the service-layers spec's open decision 2. Rejected: `@etherplay/delegation` (on-chain delegation, a different trust; its signature was found to name no chain, `docs/plans/delegation-multi-signer.md`); each service its own (the findings ask for one).
- **S1-4, GAP-3: an idempotency key on `/push`**, deduplicated per `(address, domain, key, subscription)`, so a retry reaches only the subscriptions not yet delivered; ENHANCEMENT-1, `GET /registered/:address/:domain`, with a count. Rejected: deduplicating per key only (a partial failure would then never be retried).
- **S1-5, a nodejs platform on libSQL** beside cf-worker (the dev stack and the e2e then need no wrangler), and API tests through `app.request` on in-memory libSQL, port-free, as missiv's are. Rejected: wrangler dev in the template's zellij layout.
- **S1-6, an AGENTS.md and a work/ folder** in the tree's shape (dorfl's), the findings file becoming its first closed items.
- **What the trigger service sends (S2a, 2026-11-17), checked against "As built" below by the push lane**: the bearer token, the `Idempotency-Key` header and the body as they are. One correction from the push lane, applied in S2a (a20a8b6): a `409` (the same key still sending) is retried, not dead-lettered; `502` (a send failed) is retried; `400`, `401` and `403` are permanent; the server never answers a `2xx` with `success: false`, so the adapter no longer reads one. The adapter's `url` is the server's API base (`https://<host>/api`), since the routes are under `/api`. The app registers its push subscription with the push server itself, and its trigger with the trigger service, each with the shared proof.

**Start here.**

```sh
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/push-notification-server fetch --all --prune
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/push-notification-server log --all --oneline -3   # 1e0d908 on 2026-11-17
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/push-notification-server show origin/main:docs/findings/trigger-service-integration.md | head -40
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/push-notification-server show origin/main:packages/server/src/api/index.ts | sed -n 55,150p
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/missiv show origin/main:packages/common/src/index.ts | sed -n 200,230p
```

**Do** (a branch `tf-S1/main`; one commit per item, changesets as the repo's convention asks): 1. API tests first, red on the gaps; 2. S1-3 the shared package (in missiv, its own preview branch `tf-S1/main` there, touching nothing of missiv but the extraction, since the messaging lane re-specifies the rest of missiv), published version noted; 3. S1-1, S1-2, S1-4; 4. S1-5; 5. S1-6; the client example updated to the new `/register`.

**Done when**: `/push` refuses without a domain's token and with another domain's; `/register` refuses a subscription for an address whose proof does not recover it; a retried `/push` with the same key delivers nothing twice (tested with a recording sender); `GET /registered/:address/:domain` answers a count; the nodejs platform starts and its routes pass the same tests; no test needs a port; every push and publish approved.

**As built (2026-11-17, the contract S2, F2 and S3 code against; revised the same day: missiv-identity became delegated-identity, and the packages moved under @push-notification)**: `POST /api/push {address, domain, message, topic?, urgency?}` with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (the operator configures `PUSH_TOKENS="<domain>:<sha256 hex of the token>,..."`) and, from a caller that retries, `Idempotency-Key: <key>` (a header, the IETF httpapi draft's, at most 256 characters, remembered 7 days); it answers 200 (every live subscription has the push), 502 (a send failed: retry with the same key), 409 (another request with the key is still sending: retry later), 400 (a bad key), 401 (no or unknown token), 403 (another domain's token), with `{success, delivered, alreadyDelivered, inFlight, failed, removed}`; it never answers 2xx with `success: false`. `POST /api/register {address, domain, subscription, ownershipSignature}` and `POST /api/unregister {address, domain, endpoint, ownershipSignature}` carry the header `SIGNATURE: <timestamp ms>:<compact hex>:<recovery bit>`, the delegate key's signature over delegated-identity's request envelope (this server's origin, the method and path, the timestamp, the body's keccak256; this server's origin is env `PUBLIC_ORIGIN`, else the request URL's); 401 without it or a minute off, 400 malformed, 403 not recovered (another account, another app origin, another key, another server or route, a changed body); keyed by the wallet address. `domain` is a bare host (`game.example` = `https://game.example`, `localhost:5173` = `http://`) or an origin (`http://192.168.1.10:5173`); subscriptions, tokens and deliveries are keyed by the origin it names. `GET /api/registered/:address/:domain` with the domain token: `{success, registered, count}`. S1-3's package is **delegated-identity 0.1.0** (its own repo, `github.com/wighawag/delegated-identity`, MIT; it began as missiv-identity, now deprecated; the envelope's purpose line is `delegated-identity request v1`): `verifyRequest({serverOrigin, method, path, body, signature, appOrigin, address, ownershipSignature}, {now?, maxAgeMs?})` answers `{ok: true, address, publicKey, timestamp}` or `{ok: false, reason: 'malformed' | 'expired' | 'not-authorized'}`; beside it `signRequest({serverOrigin, method, path, body, timestamp?}, delegatePrivateKey)`, `recoverRequestSigner`, `requestMessage`, `recoverAccount({appOrigin, publicKey, ownershipSignature})`, `delegatePublicKey`, `originPublicKeyPublicationMessage`, `fromDomainToOrigin`, `REQUEST_SIGNATURE_HEADER`, `DEFAULT_MAX_REQUEST_AGE_MS`. The packages are **@push-notification/server**, **@push-notification/cli** (the Node.js platform, command `push-notification-server`), **@push-notification/cf-worker** and **@push-notification/client** (MIT), all 0.1.0. The browser side is @push-notification/client: `createPushNotifications({serverEndpoint, vapidPublicKey, domain, account, serviceWorker, signOwnership?})`, a Svelte-compatible store (sveltore) of `unsupported | no-account | loading | denied | off | unregistered | on` with `enable()`, `disable()`, `refresh()`; `account` is `{address (the wallet), delegatePrivateKey, ownershipSignature?}`. In process (S2b's target): `createNodeServer({db, env, getPushSender?})` from `@push-notification/cli`, its app's `request`.

### Step S2: the trigger service, an etherfold consumer with push and reward actions

A new repo (its name decided below). Needs S1 (its push target) and F1a (the greetings processor it is tested on).

**Split into S2a and S2b (decided by the user on 2026-11-17: "ok"; so the triggers lane can start now)**: S2a is items 1 to 5 below, tested on a processor of the repo's own (a minimal test contract's event folded by an in-process etherfold server), the push adapter written against S1-1's domain token and S1-4's idempotency key with a recording target, and registration of a subscription trigger taking a proof through a seam (a test verifier) rather than S1-3's package; it needs S1's decisions taken, not S1's code. S2b is item 6 and the seam's real verifier on S1-3's package, run against push-notification-server's nodejs platform in-process; it needs S1 and F1a pushed. S2a's "Done when" is S2's below with the greetings replaced by the test processor's event and the recording push target standing for the server; S2b's adds the greetings, S1-3's proof and the real server's routes. Rejected: waiting for S1 and F1a (the trigger service's core, the larger half, depends on neither). It is the service that wires etherfold to the push server, and, by the user's refinement of 2026-11-16, it is GENERIC: "It does not exist and could be made generic to also support achievements with rewards." So its first two action types are a push notification (F2's) and an achievement with a reward (F4's later use: a high score, a badge), over one core.

**What decides the shape**: etherfold ADR-0005 (triggers run outside the indexer-server: N independent consumers, each with its own database, cursor, gate, outbox and auth), ADR-0007 (a gate: safe at finality, or fast at a depth, cancelling on retraction; a push tolerates the fast lane), ADR-0009 (an action is an authenticated HTTP call with a semantic idempotency key and a per-type delivery policy: a push expires, bounded retries then a recorded dead letter), ADR-0011 (bounded by rate, digests), ADR-0012 (triggers expire unless renewed, Web Push liveness through ENHANCEMENT-1), ADR-0013 (conditions are data over DECODED arguments, predicates and actions are operator code; subscription triggers, whose recipient is the registrant, and global rule triggers). etherfold serves `GET /{indexer}/canonical?gate=` (`packages/server/src/api/feed.ts:232`) and `GET /{indexer}/feed` (`:135`) from `etherfold run`, publicly; it stores nothing per consumer.

**Decisions** (decided by the user on 2026-11-16: as proposed, with S2-1 REFINED: "It does not exist and could be made generic to also support achievements with rewards"):

- **S2-1, its home: a new repo, `wighawag/etherfold-triggers`** (the name the user's to change; SUPERSEDED by step S2c on 2026-11-18: the service moved into etherfold as its own packages, MIT), AGPL-3.0, with a nodejs platform on libSQL first, GENERIC over its actions (refined by the user, 2026-11-16): one core (the consumer loop on etherfold's feed, the trigger registry, the outbox, the per-type delivery policy of ADR-0009) and action adapters behind one seam, an action being an authenticated HTTP call with a semantic idempotency key. Rejected: inside the push server (push is one action among rewards and mints); inside etherfold (ADR-0005 reverses exactly that); inside jolly-roger (a service in a template, against R3's "open source and self-hostable" being the service's own repo); a push-only notifier first and the generality later (the outbox, delivery policy and idempotency are the generic part, and retrofitting a second action type onto a push-shaped core is the rewrite ADR-0009 exists to avoid).
- **S2-5 (added with the refinement), the two archetypes of ADR-0013 both built**: SUBSCRIPTION triggers, registered by a user, whose recipient is the registrant ("notify me of new greetings", F2); and GLOBAL RULE triggers, defined by the operator over all events, whose recipient is COMPUTED from the event or the state as of its block ("award an achievement to whoever records a top-10 score", F4's later use). They share the matcher (data over decoded arguments) and differ in who may register and how the recipient is resolved. Rejected: subscription triggers only (the achievement is a global rule, and the matcher's index is shaped by both).
- **S2-6 (added), the reward action**: an authenticated, idempotent call to an operator-run REWARD service (its URL, token and payload schema the operator's), on the safe lane only (ADR-0007: an achievement must never be wrong and can wait), retried for days then dead-lettered (ADR-0009's per-type policy), and never a transaction submitted by the trigger service itself (ADR-0009's rule: a mint or a token transfer is the reward service's, which owns its transaction manager and is itself consumer-shaped). In this step the reward target is a recording test double, and no reward service is built. Rejected: the trigger service holding a key and minting (nonce management, gas and reorg exposure inside a delivery loop); building a reward service here (no feature needs one yet: F4-8 defers the achievement).
- **S2-2, the safe lane first** (`/canonical` at a gate of finality), the fast lane later. Rejected: fast first (cancel-on-retraction and a pending buffer, for a demo whose local chain has no reorgs).
- **S2-3, the first trigger type is a subscription trigger "a log of this event at this contract, from anyone but me"**, registered with S1-3's proof, renewed by the client, expiring otherwise; the action is `POST /push` with S1's domain token and the key `<chainId>:<blockHash>:<logIndex>:<recipient>`. Rejected: a global rule notifying every subscriber of the domain (the server would then need a broadcast it does not have, and ADR-0013 says a recipient set is data).
- **S2-4, tested without a port**: the consumer against an in-process etherfold server (`@etherfold/server`'s app with `app.request`, as its own tests do) and a recording push target. Rejected: an `etherfold run` process in the tests.

**Start here.**

```sh
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/etherfold fetch --all --prune
for a in 0005-triggers-run-outside-the-indexer-server 0007-consumers-gate-themselves-safe-and-fast-lanes 0009-actions-are-idempotent-http-calls-with-per-type-delivery-policy 0011-bound-triggers-by-delivery-rate-and-deliver-digests 0012-triggers-expire-unless-renewed 0013-trigger-conditions-are-data-actions-are-code; do git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/etherfold show origin/main:docs/adr/$a.md | head -30; done
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/etherfold show origin/main:work/specs/dropped/trigger-system.md | sed -n 1,60p
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/etherfold show origin/main:packages/server/README.md | sed -n 98,190p
```

**Do** (S2a: items 1 to 5; S2b: item 6 and the real proof): 1. the repo set up: it EXISTS (created 2026-11-17, public, `github.com/wighawag/etherfold-triggers`, cloned at `~/dev/github/wighawag/etherfold-triggers`, `main` 9986594 holding only a README and the AGPL-3.0 license); add an AGENTS.md and dorfl's `work/` as the tree's repos have, the pnpm workspace, and its `verify` in `dorfl.json`; 2. the consumer loop on `/canonical` with its cursor and outbox in one transaction, test-first; 3. the trigger registry for both archetypes (S2-3, S2-5), with expiry for subscriptions; 4. the action seam and its delivery policy (TTL, retry budget, dead letter, all recorded states), then the push adapter and the reward adapter (S2-6); 5. a nodejs CLI and the README (how an operator defines a global rule and a reward target); 6. a test end to end on GreetingsRegistry's processor (F1a's bundle) folded by an in-process etherfold server: a subscription trigger notifying, and a global rule ("every account's third greeting") calling the recording reward target.

**Done when**: a greeting by one account produces exactly one push call per subscribed other account, keyed as S2-3 says, across a restart of the consumer (cursor and outbox durable) and a retried target; an expired subscription produces none; the global rule calls the reward target exactly once per qualifying account, with its idempotency key, only from the safe lane, and a target failing past its budget ends dead-lettered and inspectable; a rewind answered by `/canonical` rolls the outbox back; no test needs a port; the repo's verify passes; every push approved.

**Learned in S2a (2026-11-17), for S2b**: GreetingsRegistry's event is `MessageChanged(address indexed user, string message)` (jolly-roger `main`), with no count in it, so "every account's third greeting" needs a predicate that reads the processor's state AS OF the log's block (etherfold ADR-0011), which S2a did not build: its test rule reads a count the test contract's event carries. S2b adds that read as a helper over etherfold's `/graphql` with `block: {hash}` (or the processor's own store in process), and needs F1a's greetings processor to keep a per-account count as a state field. The registration verifier is a seam in S2a (`RegistrationVerifier`, verifying `{path, body, headers}`; a test verifier stands in), so S2b writes its adapter on S1-3's package and nothing else changes. Its adapter is **delegated-identity**'s, at `^0.1.1` (github.com/wighawag/delegated-identity, MIT, published; it replaces missiv-identity, now deprecated, with the same API, its envelope's purpose line `delegated-identity request v1`; 0.1.1 depends only on @noble/hashes and @noble/secp256k1, the same API and wire format; @etherplay/connect-core re-exports its publication message, so the text has one source; the push lane, 2026-11-17): `verifyRequest({serverOrigin, method, path, body, signature, appOrigin, address, ownershipSignature}, {now?, maxAgeMs?})`, answering `{ok: true, address, publicKey, timestamp}` or `{ok: false, reason: 'malformed' | 'expired' | 'not-authorized'}`; a client signs with `signRequest({serverOrigin, method, path, body}, delegateKey)` into the `SIGNATURE` header. The seam is handed `{method, path, rawBody, body, headers}` (widened in S2a, 5733400, because the signature covers the raw body's hash, which a re-serialised body would not match); the adapter adds the trigger service's own public origin (configured, as the push server's `PUBLIC_ORIGIN`) and the app origin (`fromDomainToOrigin(domain)`). S2b checks the push adapter against the real server in process through `createNodeServer({db, env, getPushSender?})` and its app's `request`, with a recording `getPushSender`; it is imported from **@push-notification/cli** 0.1.0 (published beside @push-notification/server, /cf-worker and /client; the wire contract unchanged). etherfold-triggers already names delegated-identity rather than missiv-identity (854a622 on `main`, pushed 2026-11-17, approved by the user).

**As built in S2b (2026-11-18), the contract F2 codes against** (etherfold-triggers `tf-S2b/main`, on `main` 854a622):
- **Registration** (`delegatedIdentityVerifier({serverOrigin, domain, clock?, maxAgeMs?})`, exported by `@etherfold/triggers` and re-exported by `@etherfold/platform-nodejs-triggers`, since S2c; `etherfold-triggers` and `etherfold-triggers-nodejs` in S2b): `POST /subscriptions {address, domain, ownershipSignature, kind, filter?}`, `/subscriptions/renew`, `/subscriptions/cancel {.., kind, filter?}` and `/subscriptions/list`, each with the header `SIGNATURE: signRequest({serverOrigin: <the trigger service's public origin>, method: 'POST', path, body: <the exact bytes>}, delegateKey)`. The SAME identity and body shape as push-notification-server's `/register` (`{address, domain, ownershipSignature}` plus the route's fields), so an app's one delegate key and one ownership signature serve both. ONE app per service (`domain`, a bare host or an origin as the push server takes it, normalised to its origin): a subscription records no app and the push action pushes under one domain, so a second app's users would be pushed under the first's; a proof for another app is refused. `serverOrigin` must be an origin with no path (refused when the verifier is built), and the API is reached at its root with no path rewritten, since the client signs the path the service sees. Each signed request is accepted ONCE (remembered in the service's database, `accepted_requests`, until its signature expires), so a replay cannot undo a later cancel. A refused proof is `401 invalid-proof` with the reason (`no SIGNATURE header`, `the body needs address, domain and ownershipSignature` (in the code with backticks), `<origin> is not the app this service serves`, `expired`, `not-authorized`, `malformed`, `replayed`); a body that is not a JSON object is `400 invalid-body`.
- **The state read** (`stateReader({url, fetch?})`): `state.query(document, log, variables)` posts to `<url>/graphql` (the etherfold server the consumer follows, `etherfold run`'s unnamed `/graphql`) with `block: {hash: log.entry.blockHash}` as `$block`. A read that cannot answer throws `StateReadError` (`code`: a GraphQL code such as `block-not-recorded` or `block-not-retained`, `http-<status>`, `no-answer`, `invalid-answer`), so the page is not committed and is read again next round. The state is as of the END of the block. It is pinned to a block, not to the feed page's generation: across a promotion the page in flight may be matched against the successor's state, then the consumer pauses (`generation-changed`). A read that keeps failing holds the consumer at its page; the journal records `stalled` (once per distinct failure) and `recovered`. The indexer must keep the history the safe lane reads (etherfold's default `unbounded`; `--retention revert-only` has pruned it).
- **The greetings rule as an operator writes it**: `predicate: async (log, account) => ((await state.query(GREETINGS_OF, log, {account})).greeter[0]?.greetings ?? 0) >= 3` (the README's example, written out over two statements) over `query GreetingsOf($block: BlockAddress!, $account: String!) { greeter(where: {account: {eq: $account}}, first: 1, block: $block) { greetings } }` (F1a's entity, the account LOWER-CASE), key `third-greeting:<account>`.
- **The end to end** (`packages/triggers/test/greetings.test.ts`): F1a's processor copied from jolly-roger `with/indexer` c83f2f6e (`test/fixtures/greetings-processor.ts`, byte for byte but its ABI's type), folded by an in-process `@etherfold/server` whose fold writes into its canonical generation's namespace so `/graphql` answers; push-notification-server's `createNodeServer` in process with a recording Web Push sender per device; real wallets and delegate keys. No port.
- **Published by S2c**: in S2b `etherfold-triggers` and `etherfold-triggers-nodejs` were `0.0.0` with no release workflow; S2c (decided 2026-11-18) moved them into etherfold as `@etherfold/triggers` and `@etherfold/platform-nodejs-triggers`, released at `0.1.0` through etherfold's pipeline, so F2's dev stack (F2-5) installs `@etherfold/platform-nodejs-triggers` and runs its `etherfold-triggers` command.
- **A defect found upstream**: `@push-notification/server` 0.1.0 (and so `@push-notification/cli` 0.1.0) imports typia 7's runtime without depending on it, so a fresh install (npm or pnpm) resolves typia 12 and fails to import (`Cannot find module .../typia/lib/internal/_validateReport.js.mjs`); `npx push-notification-server` does not start. etherfold-triggers pins `typia` 7.6.4 as a dev dependency to run it in its tests (its finding `push-notification-server-0-1-0-needs-typia-7`). The fix is push-notification-server's: `typia ^7.6.4` a dependency of `@push-notification/server`, and a cli release taking it (the cli pins the server at `0.1.0` exactly). **Fixed since**: `@push-notification/cli` 0.1.2 (with `@push-notification/server` 0.1.2) no longer uses typia, and a clean npm install of it imports (verified by the user, 2026-11-18); S2c moved the trigger service to `^0.1.2`, removed the pin and deleted the finding.

### Step S2c: the trigger service moved into etherfold

Repo: etherfold (from etherfold-triggers `origin/main` 1aaf80f). Needs S2b. The triggers lane.

**What decided it (the user, 2026-11-18)**: etherfold's ADR-0005 says triggers do not RUN inside the indexer-server; it says nothing against the reference trigger service living in etherfold as its own packages, running as its own process. Living there gives the `@etherfold` npm org, tests against etherfold's own source (a server or feed change that breaks the consumer fails etherfold's verify, not a later release), and etherfold's existing release pipeline (changesets, `.github/workflows/release.yml` with npm OIDC trusted publishing, `scripts/reserve-npm-names.mjs`). It replaces S2-1's home.

**Decisions** (decided by the user on 2026-11-18):

- **S2c-1, moved with its history**: etherfold-triggers' commits rewritten into etherfold's layout (git filter-repo in a throwaway clone) and merged in with `--allow-unrelated-histories`, so `git log --follow` and `git blame` reach them. Rejected: one squashed import commit.
- **S2c-2, the names**: `packages/triggers` is `@etherfold/triggers` (was `etherfold-triggers`), `platforms/nodejs-triggers` is `@etherfold/platform-nodejs-triggers` (was `etherfold-triggers-nodejs`, beside `@etherfold/platform-nodejs` and `platform-nodejs-fetcher`), its command still `etherfold-triggers`.
- **S2c-3, MIT** like the rest of etherfold (relicensed by its author), no AGPL text left in what moved.
- **S2c-4, an etherfold ADR** recording that the reference trigger service lives in etherfold as its own packages and runs as its own process, complementing ADR-0005 rather than superseding it; etherfold-triggers' AGENTS.md rules and CONTEXT.md vocabulary folded into etherfold's CONTEXT.md and docs and the packages' READMEs; the idea note `what-the-first-build-left-out` moved into etherfold's `work/`.
- **S2c-5, released through etherfold's pipeline at 0.1.0 for both, by ONE changeset**; nothing published by the session: the name reservation and the trusted publishers are the user's, then the push of etherfold `main` runs the release workflow.
- **S2c-6, `@push-notification/cli` at `^0.1.2`**, which no longer uses typia: the `typia` 7.6.4 pin removed and the finding `push-notification-server-0-1-0-needs-typia-7` deleted.
- **S2c-7, the plan**: this section, the Steps list, the lanes table, S2's "As built in S2b" and F2's "Since S2b" pointing at the new packages.
- **Later, for the user's approval (not done in S2c; DONE 2026-11-18 differently: the repo deleted, see S2c's last log entry)**: etherfold-triggers itself untouched; a README pointer there to the new home, then the repo archived on GitHub.

**Done when**: etherfold's full verify (its `dorfl.json` `verify`, docs and examples builds included) passes on `tf-S2c/main`, the two moved packages' tests passing (65 and 4 at 1aaf80f; the count may only grow), with no test opening a port; `git log --follow` on a moved source file reaches its etherfold-triggers commits; no `etherfold-triggers-nodejs`, unscoped `etherfold-triggers` package name, AGPL or typia left in what moved (outside history and changelogs); `pnpm changeset status` shows the two packages at 0.1.0; every push approved, and the packages published by the release workflow after the user's reservation and trusted publishers.

**As built (2026-11-18)** (etherfold `tf-S2c/main`, on `main` a4c312fd):

The 23 commits (authors and dates kept) merged as 81612754. The platform's README is the old operator guide (config, reward target, state read, registration, operating), the core's README new (how it works, `createTriggerService`); the root README's package table has a row for both; the idea note names its new home.

### Step F2: jolly-roger `with/push-notifications`

Repo: jolly-roger (new node). Needs F1a, S1 and S2 pushed, and S2c's packages published.

**Since S2b (2026-11-18)**: the trigger registration is S2's "As built in S2b": the same delegate key and ownership signature the push client uses, signed for the trigger service's origin with delegated-identity's `signRequest`; @push-notification/client does not do it, so F2's toggle sends that one request itself (a few lines). Two prerequisites found in S2b: a `@push-notification/server` that starts from npm (0.1.0 does not, see that paragraph), and the trigger service published for F2-5's pane (in S2b it was `etherfold-triggers` at `0.0.0`). **Since F1b (2026-11-18)**: the first is met (`@push-notification/server`, `/cli` and `/cf-worker` 0.1.2, validated by zod). The second is decided by the move: the service becomes etherfold's `@etherfold/triggers` (the core) and `@etherfold/platform-nodejs-triggers` (the `etherfold-triggers` CLI, F2-5's pane), MIT, 0.1.0 by one changeset (step S2c; etherfold `tf-S2c/main` 6a51ff52, unpushed); F2 starts once both are on npm, and depends on them as published packages, never on a checkout.

**Since S1 (2026-11-17)**: the push-notifications module is replaced by an import of @push-notification/client (S1's "As built"), fed with-local-signer's delegate key and, where `@etherplay/connect` already holds it, its publication signature as `ownershipSignature`, so F2 copies no client code; what is left of this paragraph is the service worker and the payload types. **What decides the shape**: the push-notifications module (above) speaks S1's old routes; after S1 its `/register` needs the wallet's proof and the delegate signature, which with/local-signer's delegated key provides. The service worker already shows a declarative push and follows its action (`web/src/service-worker/index.ts:369,404`; the app side `core/service-worker/index.ts:153`). The payload types are declared twice. A real Web Push needs the browser's push service (Chromium: FCM over the network), which a headless e2e on a local chain cannot reach; Chromium's DevTools protocol can deliver a push message to a service worker (`ServiceWorker.deliverPushMessage`), to be measured.

**Decisions** (decided by the user on 2026-11-16: as proposed):

- **F2-1, `with/push-notifications` stems from `[with/indexer, with/local-signer]`** (the spec's `with/notifications`, named as the user names it): the notifier follows the greetings processor F1a defines, and registration needs the delegated signer. Rejected: from `with/local-signer` alone (the processor the notifier runs would then live nowhere the app ships it); a chain under `with/indexer` (it would hand indexer adopters a signer).
- **F2-2, how an indexed event becomes a notification: in a service**, S2's trigger service following an `etherfold run` of the greetings processor, calling push-notification-server. Rejected: in the browser (the tab's indexer seeing `MessageChanged` and the service worker showing a local notification: nothing arrives when no tab is open, so it is not push); push-notification-server watching the chain (etherfold's ADR-0005 and the server's own design); the greeter's client calling `/push` for its readers (S1 closes `/push` to clients, and the client would have to know every subscriber).
- **F2-3, the module wired, not rewritten**: its `/register` call takes S1-2's proof from the local signer; the duplicated types become one export the service worker imports; its README corrected (the push listener's file); unit tests for the state machine and the service worker's push and click handlers. Rejected: replacing it with push-notification-server's example client (jolly-roger's is its descendant and further along).
- **F2-4, the UI**: a "notify me of new greetings" toggle in the demo, subscribing the browser and registering the trigger with S2, renewing it on load; R2: with `PUBLIC_PUSH_SERVER` or `PUBLIC_NOTIFIER` empty the toggle is replaced by a line saying notifications are not configured, not an error. Rejected: a global settings page (the demo has one subject).
- **F2-5, the dev stack**: three zellij panes (push-notification-server's nodejs platform, `etherfold run` of the greetings bundle, the notifier), their env in `.env`, VAPID keys generated by a script into `.env.local`. Rejected: hosted services in the template's defaults (R3).
- **F2-6, the e2e**: the subscription stubbed in a fixture (`pushManager.subscribe` returning a fixed endpoint), the push server in a recording mode, so the case asserts: the registration stored, a greeting by a second account producing one recorded `/push`, and the service worker showing it when the recorded payload is delivered through `ServiceWorker.deliverPushMessage` (if item 1 measures that it works headless; otherwise by posting the payload to the worker's handler). Rejected: a real push service (network, credentials, flake).

**Start here.**

```sh
for r in jolly-roger push-notification-server; do git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/$r fetch --all --prune; done
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/jolly-roger rev-parse --short origin/with/indexer origin/with/local-signer
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/jolly-roger show origin/main:web/src/lib/core/service-worker/push-notifications/README.md | head -40
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/jolly-roger show origin/main:web/src/service-worker/index.ts | sed -n 250,300p
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/push-notification-server log --oneline -5 origin/main
ss -ltn | head -40
```

**Do** (preview branch `tf-F2/with/push-notifications`, merged from both stems; one commit per item): 1. measure headless push delivery (F2-6) in a throwaway page; 2. F2-3, test-first; 3. F2-4; 4. F2-5; 5. F2-6 and the full e2e; 6. the node in `fanout.config.json`, `README.push-notifications.md`.

**Done when**: the node merges both stems with no edit to either's files beyond the composition; its verify passes with the new tests; its full e2e is `with/local-signer`'s and `with/indexer`'s cases plus F2-6's case; with the services' env empty, the app runs and says notifications are not configured; the payload types are declared once; every push approved.

**As built (2026-11-19)**, what a later step relies on (jolly-roger `tf-F2/with/push-notifications`, merged from `with/indexer` c83f2f6e then `with/local-signer` 2ffb27c6; the config on `tf-F2/offshoot`):

- **Item 1, measured** (a throwaway page, Playwright's Chromium): `ServiceWorker.deliverPushMessage` (DevTools protocol) fires the worker's `push` event headless, in the headless shell and in the new headless alike; `pushManager.subscribe` is refused headless (`AbortError: Registration failed - permission denied`, no push service), so the e2e stubs it; `showNotification` works only in the new headless (`channel: 'chromium'`), and the headless shell (the e2e's) reports no notification permission whatever the context grants, so the e2e also makes `Notification.permission` read granted and asserts the IN-APP notification (the worker posts a push to a focused page instead of showing it).
- **The payload is push-notification-server's type** (`DeclarativePushNotification`, which its `/push` validates with zod), re-exported by `$lib/push/payload.ts` beside the worker's two handlers as functions (`notificationFromPush`, `notificationTarget`, tested in Node), which `web/src/service-worker/index.ts` calls. The root's unwired `core/service-worker/push-notifications/` is OMITTED here (`.offshoot-omissions`): @push-notification/client replaces it. F2-3's "the module wired, not rewritten" is superseded by S1's client, as "Since S1" said.
- **`$lib/push/notifications.ts`**, `createNotifications({config, kind, account, serviceWorker, signOwnership, storage?, fetch?})`: one store of `{status (the push client's), trigger ('unknown' | 'checking' | 'on' | 'off'), busy, error}` or `{status: 'not-configured'}`, with `enable()` (the device, then `POST /subscriptions {address, domain, ownershipSignature, kind}` at the trigger service, signed for its origin) and `disable()` (`/subscriptions/cancel`, then the device). The wallet is asked at most once, by `@etherplay/connect` 0.18 (added for this, the user's release of 2026-11-19): the ownership signature is the account's `savedPublicKeyPublicationSignature`, else `connection.getSignatureForPublicKeyPublication()`, which asks a wallet once per account and origin key and keeps the answer on the persisted account; the store keeps nothing of its own, and a load renews the trigger only with the account's signature (never a prompt). `publicationSigner` refuses a message connect does not sign (the app's domain must be connect's signing origin) or an account not signed in; `notifiedAccount` is one object per account and origin key, so connect keeping the signature mid-enable does not make the push client re-check. A smart-contract wallet's signature does not recover, so connect does not keep it, and such an account is asked on each enable and its trigger is not renewed on load. Connect ^0.18.0 is bumped at THIS node only (a divergence of `web/package.json`); its home is jolly-roger `main`, a cascade of its own (K4). `config` is `undefined` when a service is unset and `'off-browser'` in a server render (shown loading). `$lib/push/sources.ts`: the account from connect's connection, the configuration from `$env/dynamic/public` (`PUBLIC_PUSH_SERVER` the API root, `PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`, `PUBLIC_NOTIFIER` an origin; `//:port` resolved for the page), the worker's `ready` registration, `publicationSigner`. Dynamic, not static, env: `test/lib/context/fatal.test.ts` (local-signer's) mocks `$env/static/public` with the variables it knows, and a new static import fails it. **F3 reuses this identity and the device registration** (missiv pushes through the push server directly, S3-2, so a DM needs no trigger).
- **The app's trigger definitions**: `$lib/onchain/notifier/greetings.ts` (`NEW_GREETING = 'new-greeting'`, `notFrom: 'user'`, 30 days unless renewed, a push titled "New greeting" with the greeting (cut at 120 characters) opening `demo`), the app's like its processor. The kind is the one thing the app and the operator share.
- **The services**, `web/notifier/`: `build.mjs` (esbuild 0.28.2, browser platform as stratagems': the processor bundle from `$lib/onchain/indexer/processor.bundle.ts`, `source.json` from `contracts/deployments/<mode>`, and `serve.ts` with the services' packages external; every file landed by a rename), `serve.ts` (`push`, `index`, `triggers` or `all`: `createNodeServer` from @push-notification/cli 0.1.2, the `etherfold` 0.12.0 command (`run --promotion immediate --override`), `createNodeService` from @etherfold/platform-nodejs-triggers ^0.1.1 (@etherfold/triggers 0.2.0); ports 34005, 34002, 34006 and the app origin `http://localhost:5173` by default, each `NOTIFIER_*`), `keys.mjs` (the dev VAPID pair and push token into gitignored `.env.local`). Not the services' own commands, because the e2e needs a RECORDING push sender (`NOTIFIER_PUSH_RECORDING`, `GET /recorded`) and one process group. The trigger service's registration API served no CORS in 0.1.0 (a browser on the app's origin could not reach it); found here, fixed by the user in @etherfold/triggers 0.2.0 (the API answers CORS for the app its verifier serves), so `serve.ts` adds nothing around it. Type-checked by the web `check` script (`tsc -p notifier`).
- **A root bug, fixed here**: `core/service-worker/index.ts` (template-svelte's) handed the worker's whole message to `fromPushNotification`, so a push posted to a focused page showed no title and no body; found by the e2e, fixed at this node and listed, its home the root (K3).
- **The e2e**: `scripts/run-e2e-tests.sh` starts the notifier (`E2E_NOTIFIER_PUSH_PORT` 34105, `E2E_NOTIFIER_INDEXER_PORT` 34102, `E2E_NOTIFIER_TRIGGERS_PORT` 34106; a suite run beside another moves them too, as the prompt's ports rule now says) and builds the app against it; `greeting-notifications.e2e.ts` on account 6 (a seventh address in `impersonate-addresses.json`), the greeter a fresh key calling the contract. Other suites greet on the same chain, so the subscriber is pushed for theirs too: the case counts the push of ITS greeting, and that none is pushed twice.
- **The diff** (16 shared files, each in `[with/push-notifications]`): where the stems meet (`app.ts`, the demo page, `contracts/package.json`, the lockfile), the dependencies and `pnpmDepsHash`, the env (`web/.env`, `web/.env.localhost`), `dev/zellij.kdl`, `web/.prettierignore`, `web/README.md`, the service worker and the root fix, `scripts/run-e2e-tests.sh`, `impersonate-addresses.json`, `.offshoot-omissions`. Beyond the composition, then: the integration points a layer with services needs (env, panes, the e2e script) and the two service-worker files the payload's single declaration and the root bug required.

### Step S3: missiv reviewed, made safe and elegant, and API-stable

Repo: missiv. The messaging lane. **S3a, the review, is done** (2026-11-17, see the Progress log); **S3b, the server and the protocol, is done** (2026-11-17, pushed as missiv `main` 57587d5); **S3c, the client and the release, is done** (missiv `main` 045c140, published 2026-11-18 as 0.4.0 under `@missiv/*` and `missiv`; see its log entry). S3b needs S1-3's package landed in missiv (S1's push lane lands it first; the two lanes take turns in this repo) and S1-1's domain token decided (it is); S3c needs S3b. F3 needs S3c.

**RE-SCOPED by the user on 2026-11-17**: "I would not only try to make it safe, but more elegant, good idea to review its current code strongly". S3a reviewed every package and platform adversarially and recorded what it found in missiv's `work/` (on `tf-S3/main`, from origin/main 79fe3ab): 14 new observations, updates to 4 of the 5 existing ones, and one finding. The decisions below are its refreshed specification; they are PROPOSED until the user answers.

**What decides the shape** (origin/main 79fe3ab, measured 2026-11-17; each line names the missiv note that carries the evidence; "reproduced" means a port-free probe through `test/client-server`'s harness showed it):

- **Trust model, broken in six places.** A contract-gated room stores writes from a session whose authorization FAILED: the address is set before the check, the send path never reads `authorized` (`gated-room-accepts-writes-without-authorization`, reproduced). A key registered for one domain acts as the wallet in EVERY domain: the request middleware resolves the caller by key only and the domain check is a commented TODO (`request-key-acts-in-any-domain`, reproduced). `sendMessage` stores into any conversationID and recipient set the client names: a third party writes into A and B's thread, and "reject" is per free-form ID so a sender escapes it with a new ID (`send-message-trusts-client-conversation-id`, reproduced). Request signatures are `keccak(body)` with no time, route or purpose: the same signed send stored twice, and the room login signs a server-chosen challenge verbatim with a key that, through `@etherplay/connect`, is also an on-chain session account (`signed-requests-are-replayable-and-undomained`, the replay reproduced). The client takes recipients' keys from the server unchecked although the server returns the wallet's publication signature beside them, and silently sends IN CLEAR when told the recipient is unregistered (`client-trusts-the-server-for-recipient-keys`). The backdoors are live in a published package: `missiv-server-bun` ships `.env.default` with `DEV=true` as its default env, and `DEV=false` as a string also opens them (`dev-flag-fail-open-backdoors`, updated). There is no admin token anywhere to put `_initDB` or `db-reset` behind.
- **Registration**: a second key for the same wallet and domain answers `201 Registered` and is silently NOT stored, so that device is refused on every later call (`re-registering-a-new-key-keeps-the-old-one`, reproduced). The publication message is byte-identical to `@etherplay/connect-core`'s, which pre-signs it at sign-in for hosted accounts that can never sign it again, so its text is frozen; and missiv rebuilds the origin from a bare domain (`https://` for anything but localhost), so an app served over http on a LAN address cannot register (`work/notes/findings/publication-message-shared-with-etherplay-connect`).
- **Platforms**: the nodejs platform does not start at all: `createServer` calls `upgradeWebSocket` while building a leftover echo route and nodejs's throws (`nodejs-platform-cannot-start`, reproduced), its in-memory bootstrap targets a route that does not exist, its `migrate.ts` is dead. Bun's SQL batch is not a transaction (an async callback in a synchronous `bun:sqlite` transaction, `bun-batch-is-not-a-transaction`). The Cloudflare room accepts hibernatable sockets and also adds listeners that pass the client end, and stacks reach clients in production (`cloudflare-room-wires-two-websocket-dispatch-paths`, read, not run).
- **API and schema**: every action declared twice (common types and server zod schemas, the send assertion commented out, `ConversationMessage` already drifted), every error a 500 with code 5000, response shapes that differ for no reason, two clients (an unused typed Hono client and the hand-written `API`), CORS `*` with credentials, no bound on any string or array, whole histories returned on every poll (`api-shape-duplicated-and-unversioned`); no migrations, indexes that serve no query and none for the conversation list, a regex SQL splitter that works by luck (`schema-has-no-migrations-and-misaimed-indexes`).
- **The client's stores** (`.ts` on `svelte/store`, as the tree's Svelte rules want): the conversation list fetches once and never again, and counts accepted as unaccepted; the room store re-adds its listeners on stop and logs in with the previous socket's challenge; one undecryptable message makes a conversation throw on every poll forever, and a conversation closed while fetching polls forever (`client-stores-polling-and-room-bugs`, `one-bad-ciphertext-breaks-a-conversation`).
- **Tests and gate**: server 1 and client-server 7 pass (0.12 s, port-free), all on `FAKE:` auth, conversation `'1'` and key `'0xff'`, so they pass against every defect above; the client's test is `1 + 2`; nothing tests the Room, a platform, the stores, encryption or a production configuration; the end-to-end suite needs bun (absent here) and is expected to fail 4 of 6, not 5 (`end-to-end-tests-failing-drift`, updated); the verify skips three packages' formatting; `pnpm install` on pnpm 11 fails with `ERR_PNPM_IGNORED_BUILDS` and rewrites `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (`tests-prove-the-happy-path-in-dev-mode-only`).

**Decisions** (proposed by S3a on 2026-11-17 and DECIDED by the user the same day: "all ACCEPTED as you proposed them, with these refinements", the refinements written into S3-2, S3-6, S3-10, S3-12 and S3-15 below; S3-5 and S3-6 built in delegated-identity by the push lane, missiv's adoption of them is S3b's):

- **S3-1 (CHANGED), the backdoors removed, not flagged**: `FAKE:` auth and the all-zero registration signature go from the server; every test signs with real keys through a harness helper; `db-reset` and schema setup move behind an `ADMIN_TOKEN` (constant-time compare, refused when unset); `_initDB` goes, replaced by S3-7's migrations; stacks and causes reach a client only under an explicit `EXPOSE_ERRORS=true`; bun's `.env.default` loses `DEV`. Rejected: an explicit `DEV_BACKDOORS=true` (the 2026-11-16 decision: a flag that can be set ends up set in a shipped `.env.default`, as bun's `DEV=true` already is, and the dev loop needs no backdoor once the harness signs).
- **S3-2 (KEPT, refined), a push on a new DM**: after `/sendMessage`'s batch commits, for each recipient other than the sender who has not blocked the sender (S3-9), the server calls push-notification-server's `/push` for the recipient's wallet address with its domain token and the key `missiv:<domain>:<messageID>:<recipient>`, payload `{domain, sender}` and no content, through an injected notifier seam (a recording target in tests), off the request's critical path (`waitUntil` on cf-worker), configured by `PUSH_SERVER_URL` and `PUSH_TOKEN`, absent when unset. The message ID becomes collision-free first (S3-7). Rejected: through the trigger service (missiv is not on chain); a client-triggered push (S1 closes it); room messages notifying; awaiting the push inside the request (a slow push server would slow every send). **Against S1 as built** (S1's "As built" paragraph): `POST /api/push {address, domain, message}` with `Authorization: Bearer <missiv's domain token>` and `Idempotency-Key: missiv:<domain>:<messageID>:<recipient>` (under the 256-character limit for any domain a host name allows); `message` names the domain and the sender and holds no content; 200 is done, 502 is retried with the same key a bounded number of times then logged, 409 retried later, 401 or 403 logged as a misconfiguration and never retried. **Refined by the user (2026-11-17)**: missiv serves many domains and push-notification-server issues one token per domain, so the configuration is `PUSH_SERVER_URL` plus a per-domain token map, `PUSH_TOKENS="<domain>=<token>,..."` (the plain tokens, held as secrets), not one `PUSH_TOKEN`; a DM in a domain with no configured token sends no push and logs that once; the key `missiv:<domain>:<messageID>:<recipient>` stays.
- **S3-3 (CHANGED), rooms on nodejs, required**: the nodejs platform is made to start and gets rooms on `@hono/node-ws`; the in-memory room storage and rate limiter move from `platforms/bun` into `missiv-server` so bun and nodejs share them; the dev stack runs missiv on nodejs. Rejected: the 2026-11-16 fallback "bun in the dev stack if nodejs takes over a day" (nodejs does not even start today, bun is not on this machine, and the tree's dev stack is node).
- **S3-4 (CHANGED), the tests made to prove something, the bun-only end-to-end suite retired**: the client-server suite rewritten on real keys with `DEV` unset as the default, each reproduced defect a red test first; a Room suite on fake sockets (the review's probe is its shape); one platform contract suite through `app.request` run against nodejs (and bun when bun is present); the client's stores driven against the in-process server; the vacuous test deleted. `test/end-to-end` is removed, its `secp256k1` case moved into the client's tests. Rejected: fixing `test/end-to-end` (it duplicates client-server through a bun process on a port, and only bun machines can run it).
- **S3-5 (KEPT, refined), the registration on S1-3's shared package**, with three constraints for the push lane, which builds the package: the publication message stays byte-identical to `@etherplay/connect-core`'s (hosted accounts' saved signatures depend on it), so one of the two copies imports the other; the verifier takes the ORIGIN (scheme, host, port), never a domain to rebuild it from; and the package also carries S3-6's request envelope, so missiv, the push server and the trigger service verify one shape. Rejected: missiv keeping its own copy; changing the message to SIWE now (it would orphan every hosted account's saved signature; a second accepted message can come later, in connect first). **Built by the push lane (delegated-identity 0.1.0, MIT; formerly missiv-identity 0.2.0)**: the message unchanged; `recoverAccount({appOrigin, publicKey, ownershipSignature})` takes the origin; `fromDomainToOrigin` keeps an `http://` origin, and missiv-server's `/register` still passes `fromDomainToOrigin(domain)` until S3-5 makes the client send its origin. S1's extraction (missiv c3821ce) already moved missiv-server's `/register`, `setup` and `Room` onto the package and made missiv-common re-export the message and origin functions; what remains for missiv is its client (`API.ts` and the room still sign with their own `signAsync` over keccak) and S3-6's adoption.
- **S3-6 (NEW), one signed-request envelope**: every delegate-key signature is over a domain-separated message: a fixed `missiv` purpose tag, the server origin, the route, a timestamp, and the body's hash; the server refuses a timestamp outside a short window (60 s) and a repeated signature within it for non-idempotent routes. The room login signs the same envelope around the challenge. Rejected: a nonce fetched per request (a round trip per call, and state per key); raw keccak kept (replay and the cross-protocol exposure of a key that also signs on chain). **Built by the push lane (delegated-identity 0.1.0)**: `signRequest({serverOrigin, method, path, body, timestamp?}, key)` gives `SIGNATURE: <timestamp>:<compact hex>:<recovery>` over the EIP-191 hash of the lines `delegated-identity request v1`, `origin: <server origin>`, `route: METHOD path`, `timestamp: <ms>`, `body: 0x<keccak256 of the body>`; `recoverRequestSigner` answers `{ok, publicKey, timestamp}` or malformed or expired (60 s either way), `verifyRequest` adds not-authorized. The package keeps NO replay store: missiv keeps the signatures it accepted, for the window, on its non-idempotent routes (S3b item 5). Until then missiv-server keeps its raw-body recovery in `src/utils/signature.ts` (restored at f357d11, with a note) for its routes and the room login. **Refined by the user (2026-11-17)**: the envelope is delegated-identity's as published, purpose line `delegated-identity request v1` rather than a missiv tag (it names the library; the server origin names missiv); the replay store for non-idempotent routes is missiv's.
- **S3-7 (NEW), the domain bound to the key, and the schema migrated**: the caller's domain is the domain its key is registered for, and an action naming another is refused (403), the body's `domain` kept for readability and checked; the global profile (`Users.name`, `description`) becomes per-domain only. Numbered migration files applied by one `migrate()` every platform calls at start (cf-worker through wrangler's D1 migrations from the same files), the indexes the queries need, the JSON message blob's fields as columns, a ULID message ID. Rejected: keeping a global profile any domain's key rewrites; `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` kept (no deployed database would ever receive a column).
- **S3-8 (NEW), conversations the server keeps honest**: the conversationID is derived on the server from the sorted member set including the sender (the client's `getConversationID` for two, a hash of the sorted addresses for more), and a body that disagrees is refused; `toPublicKey` checked against the recipient's registered key for encrypted messages; every string and array bounded (content, names, recipients); `getMessages` and the conversation list paginated by cursor. Rejected: trusting the client's ID (the injection and the rejection escape above).
- **S3-9 (NEW), reject becomes block**: rejecting a conversation blocks its sender for that recipient in the domain (no new conversation, no push), and an unblock exists. Rejected: rejection per conversationID (a sender escapes it with a new ID).
- **S3-10 (NEW), one key per wallet and domain, replaceable**: registering an already registered key is idempotent; registering a new key with a newer publication signature replaces the old one (its ciphertexts become unreadable, and the client says so); a registration that does not take effect never answers 201. Assumption to confirm: `@etherplay/connect` derives the same origin key on every device for one account and origin (from the account's mnemonic, or from a wallet's deterministic RFC 6979 signature), so a second device normally re-registers the SAME key. Rejected: a key per device (a device table, and every send encrypted once per device: worth it only if that assumption fails). **The assumption checked by the user (2026-11-17)**: in `@etherplay/connect-core`'s `deriveOriginAccount`, a hosted account derives the origin key from the account key's deterministic signature over `originKeyMessage`, so every device gets the same key; with an EOA wallet it is the wallet's own signature of that message (`packages/etherplay-connect/src/index.ts`, near line 2030), deterministic for RFC 6979 wallets (the common ones). A smart-contract or passkey wallet may not sign deterministically: then each device derives its own key, "replace" means the newest device wins, and an older device's messages become unreadable. Accepted for now, recorded in CONTEXT.md and the trust-model ADR as a known limit, with "a key per device" as the way out if it bites.
- **S3-11 (NEW), the gated room enforced, and the rooms' core shared**: identity and authorization committed to a session only when both pass, every send checks them; the Cloudflare room keeps only the hibernation handlers, persists its identifier, and cleans up on error; room messages keep being attributed by the server's session (no per-message signature in rooms, recorded as a limit in CONTEXT.md). Rejected: signing room messages (a room is unencrypted and server-relayed by ADR-0002, and the server's session already authenticates its sender).
- **S3-12 (NEW), the client trusts no key it has not verified**: every public key, its own contacts' and every message's sender's, is checked against the wallet's publication signature before use; sending in clear is an explicit store call (`sendIntroduction`), never a fallback; sender, recipient and conversation are the AEAD's associated data; the per-message signature of the backlog task `per-message-signature-over-ciphertext` is built inside S3-6's envelope and verified on read, and that task is cancelled as superseded once built. Rejected: keeping the server as the key directory of record (the "untrusted relay" of CONTEXT.md is then false). **Refined by the user (2026-11-17)**: keys are verified with delegated-identity's `recoverAccount` (0.1.1, published, viem-free: only `@noble/hashes` and `@noble/secp256k1`); `@etherplay/connect-core` now re-exports its publication message, so the text has one source.
- **S3-13 (NEW, elegance), one schema source and typed errors**: the zod schemas move into `missiv-common` and the types are inferred from them; `missiv-client`'s `API` is typed from the same schemas; errors carry a status (400, 401, 403, 404, 409, 429) and a stable code; one response convention; the unused Hono client export, the echo route, `/api/public` hello, `migrate.ts`, `css2ts.cjs`, `utils/response.ts`, `requireLogin`, the `DEBUG` branch and the `"stc"` entries removed; CORS without credentials. Rejected: Hono's `hc` typed client as the client (it ties every consumer to the server package and Hono's type machinery).
- **S3-14 (NEW, elegance), the client's stores rebuilt on one lifecycle**: one small start/stop helper for polling and sockets that every store uses (replacing the copied `derivedWithStartStopNotifier` and the `running`/`fetching` flags), decryption per message into `{content} | {error}`, the conversation list and room bugs fixed, reconnect with a fresh challenge, server errors surfaced (the backlog task `frontend-send-message-error-handling`), each store tested against the in-process server. Rejected: patching the stores in place (the same lifecycle bug appears in all four, so a fifth copy of the fix is the wrong shape).
- **S3-15 (NEW), the verify made honest and the release**: the verify formats every package (root prettier installed, bun's and the client's `format:check`), runs the new suites with `DEV` unset, and `pnpm install` exits 0 on pnpm 11 (`allowBuilds` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` with real values, the `pnpm` field of `package.json` dropped); CONTEXT.md, `docs/architecture.md` and an ADR for the trust model (S3-6, S3-10, S3-12) updated; observations deleted as their fixes land (their discharge rule); missiv's packages released as 1.0.0, so F3 depends on `^1`. Rejected: a `/v1` path prefix (one consumer, pinned by semver, and nothing else uses missiv since stratagems dropped it); staying on 0.x (F3 would track a moving pre-release). **Decided (2026-11-17)**: 1.0.0, as proposed. **Changed by the user at S3c's push (2026-11-17)**: "I would not like 1.0.0 yet, make it 0.4.0 instead": released as 0.4.0, so F3 depends on `^0.4`; and the packages moved to `@missiv/*` (the Node.js server `missiv`), published by npm trusted publishing (OIDC) from missiv's release workflow (a caret on 0.x pins the minor: a 0.5 is a deliberate bump).
- **S3-16 (NEW), the build split in two sessions**: S3b the server and protocol (S3-1, S3-2, S3-3, S3-4's server side, S3-5 to S3-11, S3-13), S3c the client and the release (S3-4's client side, S3-12, S3-14, S3-15). Rejected: one session (about twenty commits across every package does not fit one context); a separate repo or rewrite (the review found the shape sound: a small relay with clean seams for storage and platform; what is wrong is checks that are missing, not the architecture).

### Step F3: jolly-roger `with/messaging`

Repo: jolly-roger (new node). Needs F2 and S3 pushed.

**What decides the shape**: missiv's client is `.ts` stores on `svelte/store` (registration, conversationList, conversation, room), which fits the tree's rules; identity is the wallet address bound to a delegate key, which with/local-signer holds (stratagems alpha1 passed `{address, delegatePrivateKey: localKey}`, alpha1-final `web/src/lib/stratagems/ui/missiv/missiv.ts:28-33`); registration costs one wallet signature (the publication message). Rooms are per domain (`@<domain>`), unencrypted, the last 100 messages kept; DMs are encrypted with an accept and reject flow.

**Since F2 (2026-11-19)**: `with/push-notifications` holds the identity F3 needs: `$lib/push/sources.ts`' `notifiedAccount(connection)` (the wallet, connect's origin key, its saved publication signature) and `publicationSigner(connection)`, which is connect 0.18's `getSignatureForPublicKeyPublication()`: missiv's `register(ownershipSignature)` verifies the SAME message, so it takes that signature and the wallet is not asked again (no cache of F3's own). A DM's push is missiv's own call to the push server (S3-2): the device is already registered for the wallet, so F3-3 adds NO trigger; it adds missiv's token to the push server's `PUSH_TOKENS` (`web/notifier/serve.ts` names one, the trigger service's) and the toggle's second line. F3's e2e needs its own accounts (index 7 and 8, two more addresses) and its own `E2E_*` port for missiv; `scripts/run-e2e-tests.sh` already starts the notifier.

**missiv 0.4 as built (S3c, 2026-11-17)**, what F3 codes against: `@missiv/client@^0.4` alone (it depends on `@missiv/common@^0.4`; the packages moved under the `@missiv` scope at 0.4.0, the user's decision, and the Node.js server is `missiv`). The account the registration store follows is `{address, signer: {address, privateKey, publicKey}}` (the wallet and its delegate key), or `undefined`; `createMissivRegistration({endpoint, domain, appOrigin?, account, fetch?})`, whose `register(ownershipSignature)` takes the wallet's signature over `originPublicKeyPublicationMessage(appOrigin, publicKey)` (connect's `savedPublicKeyPublicationSignature` for a hosted account) and needs the store subscribed; `openConversationList({registration, endpoint, domain, namespace, pollingInterval?, fetch?})`; `openOneConversation({registration, endpoint, domain, namespace, otherUser: {address}, markAsAcceptedAndRead?, pollingInterval?, fetch?})` (no `account`: the registration's is the reader), with `sendMessage` (encrypted to a verified key, refused otherwise) and `sendIntroduction` (clear, only on an explicit user action: the panel needs a button for it), each message `{content}` or `{error: {code: 'unverified' | 'undecryptable'}}`; `openRoom({url, registration, autoLogin?, createWebSocket?, reconnectDelayMs?})`, its messages `pending` then echoed or `failed` with the room's error. Every store works only while subscribed. Its tests (`test/client-server/test/stores.test.ts`) show how to drive each against `createServer`/`createInProcessRooms` with no port, which F3's unit tests can copy.

**Decisions** (decided by the user on 2026-11-16: as proposed):

- **F3-1, the home: jolly-roger, `with/messaging` stemming from `with/push-notifications`** (a chat is any app's, not a game's: bleeps and mandalas are candidates, and the games can take it through a second root as F1-2 does). Rejected: template-commit-reveal (games only; every non-game app would rebuild it); `with/messaging` from `with/local-signer` with an integration branch `[with/messaging, with/push-notifications]` for the notifications (the spec's fan: honest about messaging not needing the indexer, but two branches for one feature the user defined as notifying through F2; the chain carries the indexer layer into messaging, which costs its install and nothing in application source).
- **F3-2, what ships**: a global chat (the domain's room) and direct messages (conversations, accept and reject, unread), a messaging panel in the shell, a player's address opening a DM; registration asked once, on first use. Rejected: rooms per page or per contract (missiv's semi-private rooms gated by an `eth_call` are a later step, recorded).
- **F3-3, notifications for DMs only**, through S3-2; the F2 toggle gains a second subscription, "direct messages", on by default once push is on. Rejected: room messages notifying.
- **F3-4, the dev stack**: one more zellij pane (missiv on nodejs, S3-3: the `missiv` package's `missiv` command, or its `createNodeServer` in process for the e2e; env `DB`, and `PUSH_SERVER_URL` plus `PUSH_TOKENS` pointing at F2's push server), its env; the registration store takes the app's `appOrigin` (the page's origin by default); R2: with `PUBLIC_MISSIV` empty the panel says messaging is not configured.
- **F3-5, the e2e**: two pages, two accounts: a room message seen by the other; a DM sent, accepted, read; the recorded `/push` for the DM. Rejected: one page with two accounts switched (it hides the socket's behaviour).

**Start here.**
