**Decisions** (proposed and DECIDED 2026-11-22; each the decision, then what it rejects):

- **K4-1, four steps, by level, as K3 was, and SvelteKit 3 out of K4**: K4a solidity-proxy (outside the tree), K4b jolly-roger's nine nodes, K4c template-commit-reveal's nine and the four games, K4d template-zk-game, duel-in-the-dark, bleeps and mandalas. K4b needs K4a2 published (so one lockfile refresh at `main` carries the new router down), K4c and K4d need K4b pushed. SvelteKit 3 becomes **step K5**, measured and specified when its turn comes, starting at template-svelte with its last 2.x (2.70.4, Svelte 5.57.1 or later, the deprecations fixed) and carrying the svelte line's two leftovers above. Rejected: Kit 3 in K4 (a migration of every node's imports and its `svelte.config.js`, through 39 nodes, against P-2); one K4 step (four repos' worth of conflicts and the e2e of fourteen nodes in one context).
- **K4-2a, `Router10X60` fixed at its home, built for `london`, released as 0.5.3**: the two-line packing fix in `solc_0_8/router/Router10X60.sol`, and its second bug found reading it (a selector below the table's smallest makes `right = i - 1` underflow at `i = 0`, a `Panic(0x11)` instead of the fallback or `UNKNOWN_METHOD`), each test-first (a test in solidity-proxy on Hardhat's node test runner and viem, red on 0.5.2: entries 42 to 59 revert `UNKNOWN_METHOD`; a selector below the smallest panics); the production profile given `evmVersion: 'london'` (no `PUSH0`, which runs on every chain the tree targets and matches the games' pin), a `test` script; 0.5.3 is the commit with these and nothing else, so every `^0.5.2` consumer (`@rocketh/router` 0.20.x) takes it. Rejected: keeping `shanghai` (a chain without Shanghai cannot deploy the router).
- **K4-2b, two generic routers for 0.6.0, `Router` the default** (REVISED by the user 2026-11-22 after Etherscan refused the first design: "option 3 with 2 as default", "make sure we have comprehensive tests"): both take `Router10X60`'s `Routes` (now a file-level struct in `RouterRoutes.sol`, with the checks and the packing both share), check them at construction and binary-search the sig map in Yul. `Router` keeps the routes in a data contract it creates (read with `EXTCODECOPY`), so its own code is its compiled runtime with immutables and every explorer verifies it, at about `Router10X60`'s gas a call; `InlineRouter` appends them to its own code (read with `CODECOPY`), the cheapest, verified by its creation code (Sourcify, Blockscout), not by Etherscan. Rejected: one design only (either Etherscan or the gas lost); the routes generated as bytecode per deployment (unverifiable without deploy-time compilation); the Diamond (a storage read per call, mutable routes).
- **K4-2c, `@rocketh/router` defaults to `Router`** (K4a2, in rocketh): `solidity-proxy ^0.6.0`, the default `routerContract` `Router`, `InlineRouter` and `Router10X60` importable for a caller that names one (`Router` and `InlineRouter` share an ABI, `struct Routes`; `Router10X60`'s says `struct Router10X60.Routes`, so the option's type widens), a minor changeset (a new deployment's router bytecode changes, so a redeploy of an existing router-fronted deployment upgrades it), tests in rocketh's own suite; published by the user through rocketh's pipeline. Rejected: leaving the default `Router10X60` (every user meets the 60-selector cap again); `InlineRouter` as the default (Etherscan cannot verify it).
- **K4-3, the rocketh line at jolly-roger `main` on today's releases**: `rocketh ^0.26.4`, `hardhat-deploy ^2.4.0`, `@rocketh/node ^0.22.5` and every other `@rocketh/*` at its latest (`read-execute ^0.23.1`), `@rocketh/web ^0.19.34` where it is added (`integration` and below), `solidity-proxy` 0.5.3 through the lockfile; one commit, the lockfile re-resolved, contracts tests run at `main` (`pnpm --filter ./contracts test`, not in the verify) and the LAN deploy built at `integration`. Rejected: rocketh 0.26 with hardhat-deploy 2.2 as the bullet said (2.2 is three releases behind, and 2.4's peer is what 0.26.4 wants); a range bump without the lockfile (the hash and the LAN replay read the lock).
- **K4-4, `@etherplay/connect ^0.18.0` at `main`**, in the same commit's lockfile; `with/push-notifications`' entry for `web/package.json` drops the connect clause (the rest of its reason stays). Rejected: leaving it at the two push nodes (every other node prompts a wallet user once per service per load, and the push node's entry says its home is `main`).

**As built (K4a, 2026-11-22)**, on solidity-proxy's preview branch `tf-K4a/main` (worktree `~/dev/worktrees/solidity-proxy/tf-K4a/main`; the repo cloned to `~/dev/github/wighawag/solidity-proxy` from GitHub, its main checkout untouched), pushed to its `main` 89d8961; where it differs from K4-2a and K4-2b, this is what was built (K4a2 builds on these names):

- **The harness**: `pnpm test` is `pnpm compile && node --test 'test/**/*.test.ts'`, Node's own runner with its type stripping (so `hardhat.config.ts` imports its type with `import type`), on Hardhat 3.0.6's in-process chain (`network.connect()`, `{hardfork}` overridable, so the EIP-170 bounds are tested on `london`, which has no EIP-3860) through viem (a devDependency, with no retries: a revert is an answer), against the PRODUCTION artifacts in `generated/`, which is what the package publishes. The route implementations are raw bytecode (`test/utils.ts`: one answering its id, one echoing ADDRESS, CALLER, CALLVALUE and its calldata, one storing, one reverting), so no test Solidity reaches `generated/`. A `pnpm-workspace.yaml` denies esbuild's build script, which pnpm 11 otherwise stops the install on.
- **0.5.3** (f3eb1f4, the commit to publish 0.5.3 from; its 4 tests pass there and its `generated/` is reproduced byte for byte): `Router10X60`'s two packing lines; its search on `[left, right)` (an empty table and a selector below the smallest no longer underflow; `Panic(0x11)` confirmed red); its order check accepting `0x00000000` as the first selector (a third bug, found by the shared suite: `require(lastSig < sig)` from zero refused it, `NOT_IN_ORDER`; the branch was rebuilt to put this fix before 0.5.3, nothing having been pushed); every artifact compiled for `london`. Every contract's bytecode changes, so a deterministic deployment of any of them gets a new address (the CHANGELOG says so).
- **0.6.0** (89d8961): `solc_0_8/router/RouterRoutes.sol` (the file-level `struct Routes`, and the library `RouterRoutes` with `check` and `pack`), `Router.sol` (`Router`: the routes in a data contract it creates, `STOP` then the packed routes; four immutables: the data contract, the two counts, the fallback; `ROUTES_NOT_STORED` if the creation fails, `TOO_MANY_ROUTES` past 24,576 bytes of data) and `InlineRouter.sol` (`InlineRouterDispatch`, 375 bytes, whose constructor reverts `NOT_DEPLOYABLE`, and `InlineRouter`, its runtime with the routes and a 24-byte trailer appended). The checks: `MAX_IMPLEMENTATIONS_REACHED` past 256, `NO_ROUTES`, `IMPLEMENTATION_WITHOUT_CODE`, `SIGS_NOT_SORTED` (unsorted or a selector twice), `INVALID_IMPLEMENTATION_INDEX`. On a Shanghai chain both take at most 1,461 selectors with one implementation (EIP-3860: the routes are ABI-encoded at 32 bytes a selector); on `london`, `Router` 4,911 and `InlineRouter` 4,831 (EIP-170).
- **Tests**: 81 in 16 s. `routers.test.ts` runs the shared behaviour on all three routers (`Router10X60` within its 10 and 60): every route reached for 1 to 1,400 selectors and up to 256 implementations, around 48, 49, 60 and 61; the extreme selectors as routes; the fallback below, between and above the table; `UNKNOWN_METHOD`; the delegatecall context (the router as ADDRESS, the sender as CALLER, the value and 1,004 bytes of calldata kept); the router's storage written, not the implementation's; revert data bubbled; empty and short calldata; a model of the routing over 12 random route sets with random probes; then, on the two new ones, each check's revert, 256 implementations accepted and 257 refused, a fallback-only router, and the EIP-3860 bound at its exact boundary. `Router.test.ts` the data contract's exact code (and that calling it does nothing) and the router's code its compiled length; `InlineRouter.test.ts` its exact code, its bare dispatch refused, and both EIP-170 bounds at their exact boundaries on `london`; `Router10X60.test.ts` its four cases.
- **Gas** (above the 21,000 base, 60 routes over 10 implementations, the same routes behind all three): `InlineRouter` mean 3,811, max 4,021, deployed for 347,674; `Router` mean 6,780, max 7,071, deployed for 388,303; `Router10X60` mean 6,311, max 7,580, deployed for 545,779. Pinned: `InlineRouter`'s max below `Router10X60`'s, `Router`'s within 400 of it.
- **Verification on Sepolia** (the user's suggestion: the funded key of a fuzd session, `/tmp/fuzd-smoke/key.txt`, about 0.1 ETH, and the Etherscan key in infinite-gambit's `contracts/.env.local`; also checked first with Sourcify's library on a local node): the first design (routes appended, then named `Router`, 0x63feef30f2b7d6b9eee6ba3cbdeeb44b285114a6) Etherscan "Fail - runtime bytecode does NOT match", Sourcify `exact_match` by creation only, beside `Router10X60` (0x1f2f3f74e6673a62e737b85e8363ee69699cb5ae) "Pass - Verified"; then the built ones: `Router` (0xd6048571ef99f4fd5516a781e371c762cb3ccf0d) Etherscan "Pass - Verified", Sourcify `exact_match` of both runtime and creation; `InlineRouter` (0x51de5b20a28e3feede04511d336d8d88f1b0729b) Sourcify `exact_match` by creation, runtime null. Each answered a routed call.

## Notes to carry

Reminders the user asked to keep, so a later step does not lose them. A step that touches the subject reads its note and either acts on it or carries it forward.

- **The dev processor-bundle middleware is stratagems' FOR NOW (F1-12, 2026-11-16).** `stratagems/web/vite.processor-bundle.ts` (df641fc8) serves `processor.bundle.js` in dev from the indexer's watch build and reloads the page on a new bundle; without it a stale copy in `static/` left the board on "Loading the board..." for good. Any game that PUBLISHES a processor bundle needs the same thing. Its home is template-commit-reveal `with/indexer` (beside `host.ts`, which names the published bundle), with the bundle's path a parameter, as soon as a second game (or the reference game) publishes one. Checked at F1b (2026-11-18: the reference game still publishes none, so it stays), and at K3's measure (2026-11-22: only stratagems has `vite.processor-bundle.ts` or a publishing build, so it stays). Check this at F4b (high scores may make the reference game publish a snapshot) and K3, and whenever a game adds `etherfold build --publish`.

- **S1-3's package must fit missiv and @etherplay/connect (found by S3a, 2026-11-17).** missiv's publication message is byte-identical to `@etherplay/connect-core`'s `originPublicKeyPublicationMessage`, which connect pre-signs at sign-in for hosted (email, OAuth) accounts that can never sign it again, so its text is frozen and one of the two copies should import the other. The verifier must take the ORIGIN, not rebuild it from a domain (missiv's `fromDomainToOrigin` makes every non-localhost origin `https://`, which breaks an http LAN dev stack). And the package should also carry the signed-request envelope of S3-6 (a purpose tag, the server origin, the route, a timestamp and the body hash), because the delegate key it verifies is connect's origin account, which also signs on chain. Checked at S1 (the push lane builds the package) and S3b. Evidence: missiv `work/notes/findings/publication-message-shared-with-etherplay-connect.md` and `work/notes/observations/signed-requests-are-replayable-and-undomained.md` on `tf-S3/main`. **Answered (2026-11-17)**: the push lane accepted all three and builds them before its first publish, under MIT.

- **What was done**: etherfold, push-notification-server, missiv and dorfl fetched (`git fetch --all --prune`); etherfold-triggers' main checkout clean, `origin/main` 9986594 as the plan says (a README and the license). Preview branch `tf-S2a/main` from `origin/main` in `~/dev/worktrees/etherfold-triggers/tf-S2a/main`. A pnpm workspace: `packages/triggers` (`etherfold-triggers`, the core, platform-neutral over a `remote-sql` database and a `fetch`) and `platforms/nodejs` (`etherfold-triggers-nodejs`, the `etherfold-triggers` CLI on libSQL).
- **Item 1**: AGENTS.md, CONTEXT.md (the glossary), `work/` in dorfl's regime layout with `work/protocol/` synced by `dorfl sync` (protocol 2026-11-17), `dorfl.json` (`prepare` `pnpm install`, `verify` `pnpm format:check && pnpm build && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test`, `testFirst`, dorfl pinned through `dorflCmd` as etherfold does), prettier as etherfold's, TypeScript 6, vitest 4, AGPL-3.0-only.
- **Item 2, the consumer** (test first): reads `/canonical?gate=` page by page and commits the page's deliveries and the cursor in ONE `batch`; a rewind marks the undelivered rows at or above the fork block `retracted` (freeing their keys: the outbox's unique index is partial, `WHERE status != 'retracted'`), marks the already-sent ones `retracted_at`, journals it and resumes from the `rewindCursor`; a changed `generation`, or a refusal such as `stream-mismatch`, PAUSES it until `resume` (the expected behaviour the etherfold server README records). The `CanonicalFeed` seam has two adapters: HTTP over an injected `fetch` (the in-process `@etherfold/server` app's in the tests) and a scripted one for the pauses.
- **Item 3, the registry**: subscription kinds (operator-declared: the event, the filterable arguments, `notFrom` for "from anyone but me", the action, the renderer, a TTL) and global rules (recipients computed, an optional predicate, a semantic key), both over arguments DECODED by viem against operator-declared events; subscriptions matched through the inverted index `(kind, arg, value) -> subscription` of ADR-0013; registration as an upsert that is also the renewal, `renewAll` per subscriber, cancel, a per-recipient quota, and an `expire` sweep that marks and journals (never a DELETE), with an expired subscription matching nothing even before the sweep. The registration API (Hono: `POST /subscriptions`, `/renew`, `/cancel`, `/list`), every route acting for the address the proof RECOVERS, behind the `RegistrationVerifier` seam.
- **Item 4, delivery**: the `ActionAdapter` seam (a policy, the lanes it may fire from, `deliver`), the deliverer (due rows oldest first; TTL from the policy or the row; backoff `min(maxMs, initialMs * factor^(n-1))` or `Retry-After`; a dead letter past the budget or on a refusal; `requeue` for an operator; every terminal state journaled), `pushAction` (S1-1's bearer token, the `Idempotency-Key` header, a partial failure retried) and `rewardAction` (safe lane only, no TTL, 30 attempts from 30 s to 6 h, about five days; a `409` retried), `assertActionsFitLane` refusing at startup a reward on any other lane, and `createTriggerService` composing them, its `tick` delivering even when the feed is down.
- **Item 5**: the nodejs CLI (`run`, `tick`, `status`, `resume`, `deliveries`, `requeue`, `journal`), its config module (`defineConfig`), the safe lane's `rpcGate` (the `finalized` tag, or `latest - depth`), and the README's operator guide (a subscription kind, a global rule, a reward target's contract, registration, operation). Plus `work/notes/ideas/what-the-first-build-left-out.md`: the fast lane, digests, dormancy, the state read, breadth checks.
- **Measured against "Done when"** (S2a's: the test contract's `Posted(address indexed author, uint256 count, string text)` folded by an in-process `@etherfold/server`, recording targets for the push server and the reward service; `packages/triggers/test/service.test.ts` unless said): a post by one account produces exactly one delivered push per subscribed other account, keyed `<chainId>:<blockHash>:<logIndex>:<recipient>`, across a restart of the consumer on a database on disk (the process stopped after one page) and a target failing once (5 calls, 4 keys, the retry under the same key); an expired subscription produces none, and its expiry is journaled; the global rule ("every account's third post") calls the reward target exactly once per qualifying account with its key `third-post:<account>`, also across a restart, and a fourth post is absorbed by the key; `lane: 'fast'` is refused at startup because the reward action fires from the safe lane only; a reward target failing past its budget ends `dead`, with its attempts and its last error, and journaled; a rewind answered by `/canonical` retracts the undelivered deliveries from the fork block and the new branch's are delivered instead (also `consumer.test.ts`, on the server's own reorg fixture); a failed commit moves neither the cursor nor the outbox (`consumer.test.ts`). No test opens a port: the feed is the app's `fetch`, every target a recording `fetch`, the CLI's `run` the one command not exercised (its server injected). The verify passes in the preview worktree and in a fresh clone with `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`: core 7 files / 42 tests, nodejs 1 / 4, build and typecheck clean, format clean. NOT met: every push approved, so the box is not ticked.
- **Commits** (etherfold-triggers `tf-S2a/main`): 338f736 (item 1), af59839 (item 2), bcea3df (item 3), c9d2ed5 (item 4), 7481cf3 (item 5), 733fbdf (the left-out note), a20a8b6 (the push lane's correction, after S1's "As built": a `409` and a `502` retried, the `success: false` reading dropped, the `url` the API base), 5733400 (the registration verifier handed the method and the raw body as sent, what missiv-identity 0.2.0's `verifyRequest` signs; verify green again, 42 and 4 tests). jolly-roger `tf-S2a/work`: this entry and the plan's fixes.

- **Decisions taken while building** (within S3-1 to S3-16): the per-message signature stays the top-level field in S3b and moves into `messages[]` with S3-12 in S3c (one API change, before 1.0); the sender's own encrypted copy may name any member's registered key (the key its ECDH secret is with), a recipient's copy must name that recipient's; a clear copy names no key and stores none; blocked recipients get no copy and no push, silently (the sender learns nothing of the block); `register` and `sendMessage` are the routes that refuse a replay (the others are idempotent); the room's identifier is derived from the request each time a Durable Object wakes, rather than persisted (a DO is addressed by the room's name, so they cannot differ); a room's in-memory history is bounded (200) and the number of in-process rooms too (10 000, the least recently used socket-less one dropped); 1.0's first migration starts from an empty database (a 0.x database is not migrated, said in the changeset); the push's `navigate` is the recipient's app origin, its `tag` the conversation, so a newer message replaces the older notification.
- **Surprises**: the rate limiter counts a room login as a message and lifts its cooldown a tick later, so a client sending right after joining is refused once (the Room test waits a tick; worth a look in S3c's stores); `@hono/node-ws` rebuilds an upgrade's URL on `http://localhost` unless given a base URL, which made every room login over node fail until the Host header was used; cf-worker's `tsc` had a pre-existing error in `src/logging` (fixed in item 7).

- **What was done**: missiv fetched (`git fetch --all --prune`), its main checkout clean; `origin/main` 57587d5 (S3b, pushed: the S3b entry above has no "Pushed" line, but the ref is there), `tf-S3/main` at it, so no fast-forward was needed; `tf-S3/work` at `origin/work` 39b88c61 (C1a's entry), nothing to rebase. - **The commits** (missiv `tf-S3/main`): d021bbc item 9 (S3-12): each copy of a message carries its own `signature`, the sender's delegate key over an EIP-191 `missiv message v1` (domain, namespace, conversation, type, sender, recipient, key, content keccak; `signMessageCopy` and `recoverMessageCopySigner` in missiv-common), the top-level `signature` gone; the server stores it per copy with the sender's ownership proof as it was when sent (`senderOwnershipSignature`, `senderAppOrigin`, two columns in the unreleased 0001 migration) and checks neither; missiv-client's new `messages.ts` verifies every key (`verifyDomainUser`: the proof recovers the address for an origin of the domain), builds signed copies (`encryptedCopies`, `clearCopies`; the scope and both keys as the AEAD's associated data) and opens each copy on its own (`openMessage`: `{content}` or `{error: unverified | undecryptable}`); the conversation sends encrypted only to a verified key and in clear only through `sendIntroduction`; the registration is `Registered` only for its own proven key (`otherKey` otherwise). 2903279 item 10 (S3-14): `utils/lifecycle.ts`'s `activeStore` (a run per subscription and per account, a stopped run cannot write) and `poll`, the four stores rebuilt on it and `derivedWithStartStopNotifier` deleted; the list polls again and counts requests and unread apart; a conversation closed while fetching stops; `markAsAcceptedAndRead` accepts once per newer message; the room reconnects with backoff and a fresh challenge, removes its listeners, and marks a refused message `failed` with the room's error; every room error got a `code` and room messages lost their unused `signature` field (S3-11); each store takes `fetch`, the room `createWebSocket`; the demo app's svelte-check error fixed (the conversation panel takes no `account`) and svelte-check put in missiv-client's build, so the verify catches a type error. 5121400 item 11 (S3-15's docs): CONTEXT.md's vocabulary and its "untrusted relay" as built, `docs/architecture.md` (packages, configuration, trust), ADR-0003 (the client verifies, the server relays; its known limits: non-deterministic wallets, no forward secrecy, metadata, rooms), READMEs for missiv-client (it was create-svelte's), missiv-server and each platform; all 19 observations discharged (their fixes landed in S3b and S3c), two new ones (bun and cf-worker not run since the rework; the room rate limiter refusing a quick burst); the backlog tasks `per-message-signature-over-ciphertext` and `frontend-send-message-error-handling` moved to `tasks/cancelled/`, superseded by items 9 and 10. e13c35d item 12: a major changeset for the six published packages, the packages depending on each other with `workspace:^` (missiv-common a dependency of missiv-client, no longer a peer), and `prettier-plugin-svelte` at the root (without it `changeset version` cannot format missiv-client's changelog and leaves it unwritten).
- **Measured against "Done when" (S3c)**: the verify (`pnpm install && pnpm format:check && pnpm build && pnpm test`) passes: missiv-server 7, missiv-client 1, missiv-server-nodejs 3, client-server 57 (35 before: `trust.test.ts` 10, `stores.test.ts` 12), three runs of client-server alike, svelte-check 0 errors (4 warnings, the demo's Modal CSS), cf-worker's `tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck` clean; no test opens a port and no process was left. The client refuses a key whose publication signature does not recover its address (C's key under B's name, with C's proof or B's; a proof for `https://other.com`), and a conversation then encrypts nothing (`unverifiedKey`, `sendMessage` refused, nothing stored); it never sends in clear unasked (a recipient with no key: `sendMessage` refused, `sendIntroduction` sends clear); it verifies each copy's signature (a changed content, conversation or namespace, or a sender key swapped for another account's with its proof, is `unverified`); an undecryptable copy is shown as such between readable ones. Each store has tests against the in-process server: registration (Idle, Unregistered, Registered, `otherKey` then replaced, a refused register surfaced with its code), list (polls, counts, stops), conversation (both sides, stops even while fetching, accepts once, a failing server surfaced then recovered, a new account replaces the run), room (login, send, echo; a rate-limited message `failed`; reconnection with a fresh challenge and no socket left after unsubscribe; read-only without an account). The docs and ADR-0003 describe the trust model built. 1.0.0 is ready to publish: `changeset version` in a throwaway worktree gives all six packages 1.0.0 with their changelogs, and `pnpm pack` of missiv-common, missiv-client, missiv-server and missiv-server-nodejs gives `missiv-common: ^1.0.0` and `missiv-server: ^1.0.0` dependencies (the throwaway worktree and the tarballs removed). NOT met: every push and publish approved, so the box is not ticked. NOT verified (item 11's last clause): the bun and cf-worker platforms run once, as there is neither bun nor wrangler here; recorded as missiv's observation `bun-and-cf-worker-platforms-not-run-since-1-0`.
- **Decisions taken while building** (within S3-12, S3-14, S3-15): the copy signature is missiv's own EIP-191 message (`missiv message v1`), not delegated-identity's request envelope (a message is not a request: no server or route, and it outlives the 60 s window), its format `0x` + compact + recovery byte, so it fits the schema's hex; each copy stores the sender's ownership proof as at sending, so a reader verifies a message after its sender replaced the key (rejected: the sender's current key fetched at read time, which would orphan every older message); the two columns went into 0001 rather than a 0002, since 1.0 is unreleased and starts from an empty database; the associated data binds the domain, namespace, conversation and both ECDH keys (the same for the two copies of one ciphertext); `openOneConversation` lost its `account` parameter (the registration is the reader) and an `otherUser.publicKey` given by the caller is ignored (only a key the store verified is used); the room opens a new socket on an account change instead of a logout and login on the old one (a fresh challenge, and one state machine less); a pending room message is resolved by the first echo from our address with the same text (the room relays one socket in order), and an error fails our oldest pending one; the two superseded backlog tasks went to `tasks/cancelled/` (with a `reason:`) rather than `tasks/done/`, since they were built as parts of S3c's items, not as their own tasks; the release is the changesets alone: the `changeset version` commit and `changeset publish` are made at publish time, on approval, so the push carries no version bump.

Fixed in b7cb2ee6: `conversationView` is undefined until the inbox is fetched (the panel shows loading), with a test on a `Fetching` list (red on the old line). Same commit, three nits: `register()`, `accept` and `reject` read the registration itself (`get`) rather than its last subscribed value; a signature asked for an account or key no longer in use is dropped instead of being sent to missiv; `serve.ts`' header counts four services; and `README.messaging.md` says why the app is opened at `localhost` (missiv names the app by its bare host, and the push server reads a bare http host other than `localhost` as `https://`, so a DM there would push nothing). Not done: the demo's opener also shows on the account's own greetings (it opens a conversation with oneself, harmless). Etherscan probably cannot verify it; Sourcify can.
